## Naming Rule Summary

| Scope | Rule ID | Convention |
|---|---|---|
| `packages/react/src/*/*.tsx, !packages/react/src/ui/**, !packages/react/src/fields/**, !packages/react/src/fields/*.tsx, !packages/react/src/forms/**, packages/next/src/**/*.tsx, packages/richtext-plate/src/**/*.tsx` | react-components-pascal | React component files are PascalCase named after their default export; `create*` factories and `resolve*` component resolvers (functions returning a `ComponentType`, composing an override chain with JSX) keep their camelCase names. Exception (developer, 2026-11-02) — `fields/registry.tsx`, the field-type registry module. See /dev/naming-conventions/#react-components-pascal. |
| `packages/react/src/ui/**` | shadcn-ui-kebab | shadcn-generated primitives keep their kebab-case names (button.tsx, alert-dialog.tsx); hand-written additions in ui/ may be PascalCase (ThemeProvider.tsx, data-table/DataTable.tsx). Hand-written non-component `.ts` helpers in ui/ may be camelCase (resizeHandleStyles.ts, data-table/DataTable/dataTableCellClasses.ts — developer exception, 2026-11-02). See /dev/naming-conventions/#shadcn-ui-kebab. |
| `packages/react/src/hooks/**` | hooks-camel-use | Hook files are camelCase named after the exported hook. shadcn-generated hooks (use-mobile.ts) keep kebab-case. See /dev/naming-conventions/#hooks-camel-use. |
| `packages/core/src/fields/*/**` | core-field-module | Each core field dir (lowercase widget name) contains config.ts, types.ts, validator.ts, inputSchema.ts, with colocated .test.ts files. See /dev/naming-conventions/#core-field-module. |
| `packages/react/src/fields/*/**` | react-field-module | Each react field dir mirrors the core field name and contains Field.tsx, Cell.tsx, columnDef.tsx (+ index.ts barrel), plus testFixture.ts exporting that field type's FieldFixture and any field-specific test helper. Complex fields (date, color, json) may also hold a utils.ts of field-local helpers and PascalCase helper widget components (upload/relationship/richtext's EmptyInput/FilledInput/RelationshipPicker); layout fields (tabs/row/collapsible/ui) hold Field.tsx (+ Field.test.tsx) only — no Cell/columnDef, since they never appear in list views. A field type owns everything about itself in one folder; testing/fixtures/index.ts only aggregates. See /dev/naming-conventions/#react-field-module. |
| `packages/react/src/forms/**` | react-app-form | The exported app form (@vexcms/react/form) is named after each field it exports (TextField.tsx, ArrayField.tsx, BlocksField.tsx), plus the fixed roles formContext.ts, useAppForm.ts, AppForm.tsx, FormFieldLayout.tsx, types.ts. These files read useFieldContext()/useFormContext() and plain props only — never fieldDef, VexConfigContext, or permissions; that wiring belongs in fields/<type>/Field.tsx. BlocksField's `cloneBlock` prop is intentionally optional — callers that omit it simply don't get a per-item duplicate action. See /dev/naming-conventions/#react-app-form. |
| `packages/*/src/**/*.test.*` | tests-colocated | Tests are colocated next to the unit under test with a .test.ts(x) suffix — no __tests__ dirs, no .spec suffix. See /dev/naming-conventions/#tests-colocated. |
| `packages/core/src/**, packages/cli/src/**` | non-component-camel | Non-component TypeScript files are camelCase (or established lowercase names like constants.ts). See /dev/naming-conventions/#non-component-camel. |
| `packages/react/src/testing/**` | react-testing-kit-camel | The exported testing kit (factories, shared harness, the fixture registry/type) is camelCase — it ships as plain functions, not components, even where a file contains JSX. Per-field-type fixture DATA does not live here; it lives in each field's own fields/<type>/testFixture.ts. See /dev/naming-conventions/#react-testing-kit-camel. |
| `packages/core/src/*/api/**` | api-operation-split | Each domain's api/ folder holds one <op>/server.ts (+ server.test.ts) per operation, a <domain>Api.ts factory named after its export, and an index.ts that only re-exports. A types.ts holds arg/result types shared across that domain's operations. See /dev/naming-conventions/#api-operation-split. |
| `packages/cli/src/commands/**` | cli-command-verbs | CLI command files are camelCase verbs naming the action. See /dev/naming-conventions/#cli-command-verbs. |
| `apps/test/convex/*.ts, apps/test/convex/auth/**, apps/test/convex/vex/**` | convex-resource-files | Convex function files are camelCase resource/action nouns. See /dev/naming-conventions/#convex-resource-files. |
| `packages/react/src/**/*Context.*, apps/test/src/**/*Context.*, apps/www/src/**/*Context.*, packages/create-vexcms/templates/*/src/**/*Context.*` | react-context-suffix | React Context definition files are PascalCase with a Context suffix; a context with a companion hook lives instead as one hooks/useXxx.ts file. See /dev/naming-conventions/#react-context-suffix. |
| `apps/test/src/auth/**` | auth-file-roles | Host-app auth files use fixed role names separating client/server/util/config concerns. RBAC adds `access` (the `defineAccess` matrix), `hasPermission` (the app-side check wrapper), and `plugins` (the better-auth plugin list). `schema` is the client-safe half of the auth options — model names, additional fields, and the plugin descriptor map — imported by BOTH `options.ts` and `vex.config.ts` so they cannot drift; it must stay free of server imports. A role may carry a `<role>.typecheck.ts` sibling — a pure compile-time assertion file (P-008) that `tsc` enforces; it pins the role's generated/registry-derived types (e.g. `access.typecheck.ts` pins slug-aware action unions). See /dev/naming-conventions/#auth-file-roles. |
| `apps/test/src/vexcms/collections/**` | vexcms-resource-defs | Collection definitions are camelCase resource names (no PascalCase, no type suffixes). Preferred camelCase; kebab tolerated for legacy files pending rename. See /dev/naming-conventions/#vexcms-resource-defs. |
| `apps/test/src/vexcms/blocks/*/**` | vexcms-block-module | Each block is a PascalCase directory (named for the block, matching its blockType literal in title case) holding config.ts (the defineBlock() call) and index.tsx (the renderer); blocks/config.ts and blocks/index.ts at the top level only aggregate. See /dev/naming-conventions/#vexcms-block-module. |
| `scripts/**/*.mjs, scripts/**/*.sh` | script-files | Build/utility scripts are kebab-case .mjs (node) or .sh (shell). See /dev/naming-conventions/#script-files. |
| `apps/docs/src/content/docs/*.md, apps/docs/src/content/docs/*.mdx, apps/docs/src/content/docs/guides/**, apps/docs/src/content/docs/fields/**` | docs-content-kebab | Hand-written docs content files are kebab-case .mdx/.md. The api/ subtree is TypeDoc-generated (symbol-named) and exempt. See /dev/naming-conventions/#docs-content-kebab. |
| `packages/react/src/*` | react-feature-folders | The react package's top level is a CLOSED set of domain folders mirroring core/src/ (access, admin, auth, client, collections, config, documents, fields, forms, globals, hooks, livePreview, media, modals, revalidate, slots, testing, ui, utils, versions) plus adapter.ts, adminCookie.ts (client writer for core's root adminCookie.ts codec — developer exception, 2026-11-02) and index.ts. A new top-level folder is allowed only for a new domain that core/src/ also has, and is added to this list in the same change; never one named after a UI surface, view, or feature (list/, edit/, data/, views/, table/, filters/, actions/). Placement: code serving ONE domain's view lives in that domain folder with a domain/view name prefix (collections/CollectionListToolbar.tsx). Code shared by several domains' views goes in the existing domain that owns the shared concept (collection + global edit view → documents/, mirroring core/src/documents/); if none fits, ask the developer. Hooks bound to a domain stay in it (access/usePermission.ts); hooks/ holds only generic hooks reused across domains (usePaginatedQuery, useVexList). Generic UI primitives with no domain knowledge go in ui/ (ui/data-table/DataTableColumnsMenu.tsx). Inside a domain folder, files stay flat; the only subfolders are api/ (server.ts + client.ts per operation) and fields/<type>/. Once a folder grows crowded, the DEVELOPER splits it into the conventional React subfolders components/, hooks/, context/. Agents never split a folder themselves and never invent ad-hoc subfolders. `collections/` is the one exception to the flat-with-prefix rule: it holds a CLOSED set of feature subfolders — `listView/`, `editView/`, `api/` — each camelCase like `livePreview/`, each holding every component/hook/helper for that feature with the redundant `Collection` prefix dropped from new in-folder file names (listView/toolbar/ListViewToolbar.tsx); public root components keep their names (listView/CollectionListView/index.tsx, listView/create/ListViewCreateDocumentModal.tsx, editView/CollectionEditView.tsx). Within `listView/`, a cohesive AREA (toolbar/, savedListViews/, filters/, peek/, create/, rowActions/) may further group into its own `listView/<area>/` subfolder, same rule one level deeper — a new area subfolder needs the developer's approval and an addition to this list. A component outgrown into multiple files becomes a folder named after it, holding `index.tsx` (the main component) plus its split-out pieces — `index.tsx` re-exports ONLY symbols imported from outside the folder today; siblings inside the folder never import from `index.tsx` (no cycles). A new collections/ subfolder needs the developer's approval and an addition to this list. See /dev/naming-conventions/#react-feature-folders. |
| `packages/core/src/*` | core-domain-folders | The core package's top level is a CLOSED set of domain folders plus camelCase root modules (index.ts, server.ts, errors.ts, framework.ts, vexConvexApi.ts). A new domain folder is added to this list in the same change that creates it. Never add a TOP-LEVEL folder for a sub-feature of an existing domain — group it instead into a `<domain>/<feature>/` subfolder (e.g. saved views are a collections feature, so they go in `collections/savedListViews/`, not a top-level `views/`). New server operations go in <domain>/api/<op>/server.ts (api-operation-split). See /dev/naming-conventions/#core-domain-folders. |
| `apps/test/src/components/*.tsx, apps/www/src/components/*.tsx, apps/test/src/components/motion-primitives/*.tsx, apps/www/src/components/motion-primitives/*.tsx, packages/create-vexcms/templates/*/src/components/*.tsx, packages/create-vexcms/templates/*/src/components/motion-primitives/*.tsx` | host-app-components-pascal | Host-app `components/` root is PascalCase components named after their default export (mirrors react-components-pascal); `ui/` keeps the shadcn exception below, `providers/` keeps its fixed roles, and `motion-primitives/` (marketing-site derived apps only) is a feature subfolder of plain PascalCase widgets, not a components/providers-style fixed-role folder. See /dev/naming-conventions/#host-app-components-pascal. |
| `apps/test/src/components/ui/**, apps/www/src/components/ui/**, packages/create-vexcms/templates/*/src/components/ui/**` | host-app-ui-kebab | Host-app `components/ui/` mirrors packages/react/src/ui's shadcn-ui-kebab exception — generated primitives keep kebab-case, hand-written additions may be PascalCase. See /dev/naming-conventions/#host-app-ui-kebab. |
| `apps/test/src/components/providers/*.tsx, apps/www/src/components/providers/*.tsx, packages/create-vexcms/templates/*/src/components/providers/*.tsx` | host-app-providers-fixed-roles | Host-app `components/providers/` is a fixed-role folder, like auth/ — one file per provider concern (client-only context providers, server-rendered wrappers, auth session provider, Convex client provider), composed in app/layout.tsx. Not a dumping ground for arbitrary components. See /dev/naming-conventions/#host-app-providers-fixed-roles. |
| `apps/test/src/lib/**, apps/www/src/lib/**, packages/create-vexcms/templates/*/src/lib/**` | host-app-lib-camel | Host-app `lib/` holds camelCase utility functions and hooks (useXxx.ts for hooks, camelCase.ts for utilities) — no feature-folder restructure; each file is a self-contained concern (metadata.ts, highlight.ts, vex.ts). See /dev/naming-conventions/#host-app-lib-camel. |
| `packages/next/src/*` | next-adapter-layout | packages/next is a framework adapter, not a domain package — its root holds the primary `Next*.tsx` components (NextAdminPage, NextAdminLayout, NextAdminLayoutClient) plus index.ts, and capability folders (cache/, seo/, testing/) group by what they do rather than mirroring core/react's domain structure. Codified as-is per spec's pass-2 review (next has 0 violations). See /dev/naming-conventions/#next-adapter-layout. |
